# SQLite migration progress

## Increment 1 — valid canonical seed and correction prerequisites

Original specification: `SQLITE_MIGRATION_PLAN.md`. Full scope remains active; this increment does not complete Phases 2–4.

- Added a reusable diagnosis seed constructed with supported task creation, discovery, contract creation, routing, preflight, activation, phase and check commands. Each boundary validates ledger/state coherence. The failing check is an actual child process with exit code 1.
- The same valid snapshot continues through selected SQLite diagnosis/correction dispatch without operational export or reseeding between commands.
- Filesystem and SQLite now share the execution-prerequisite evaluator and preflight rules through internal read adapters. External observations are gathered outside the write transaction; a task/artifact/ledger fingerprint is rechecked inside it before mutation.
- Missing persisted preflight yields the same rejection on both backends without dropping the committed diagnosis.
- Canonical SQLite advance rolls back after injected state-write and event-write failures.
- Existing continuity tests use the real lifecycle seed and assert revision increments relative to the seed instead of assuming revision zero.

Targeted validation: 11 tests passed on Node 26.10.0/macOS. Prior `verify:fast` passed before the prerequisite adapter changes; current full storage regression is running. ESLint reports no errors and four complexity warnings in shared prerequisite/preflight functions; final simplification remains required.

## Baseline and inventory

Source identity: `ee9ce11123d4e728d3dbc92f5d62d4bf41bb79c5`, plus copied existing SQLite work and this increment. All baseline scenarios remain distinct from final equal-work comparisons.

The existing task-discovery benchmark observed 10/100/250 tasks and returned first/warm timings of 25.441/11.426 ms, 112.111/106.106 ms, and 281.261/259.324 ms. These are single-run harness measurements, not p95 values or controlled filesystem-cold results. Its task fixtures do not cover the required final valid-history workload.

`SQLITE_MIGRATION_MATRIX.md` lists every discovered command/MCP surface and its pending consumer audit. The reproducible inventory script records direct named I/O/persistence signals and transitive local dependencies; discovery is not coverage authority.

## Remaining scope

1. Finish Phase 2C complete valid/invalid parity, independent-process contention and attempted-I/O observation.
2. Preserve byte-bound operational artifact evidence in the importer/store; migrate optional prerequisite readers and eliminate legacy fallback.
3. Migrate bootstrap, lifecycle edges, claims/recovery, actions/approvals, all artifacts and public read surfaces.
4. Implement explicit migration, exclusion/cutover recovery, backup/restore and downgrade boundaries.
5. Set and verify the breaking Node 24+ runtime/storage compatibility boundary.
6. Remove superseded filesystem persistence machinery; measure equivalent-work p95, memory/contention and net LOC against the original targets.
7. Run full required verification, complete the ForgeLoop task and open the PR. No release or live rollout is authorized.

## Increment 2 — semantic parity, ownership and snapshot export

- Shared receipt preparation, claim-evidence construction and claim classification now serve both continuations. Store guards reject extracted-column/payload disagreement.
- Diagnosis and correction dispatch include canonical command commit witnesses. Correction emits no invented phase milestone. Valid sequence comparison covers normalized state, receipt and events. Missing-preflight and real recovery cases reject without mutation.
- Decision artifact validation reads the selected store. Original accepted artifact source bytes and digests survive import/export in schema version 2, storage-format version 1. Future metadata is not downgraded.
- Independent correction workers and both absent/contradictory legacy-file attempted-I/O scenarios pass. Injected after-state/after-event failures preserve the prior diagnosis.
- Export captures a private read-only SQLite backup snapshot before portable filesystem writes. Ordered ledger rows stream to the atomic writer. Manifests bind file size and SHA-256; unsafe identities and symlink destinations reject.

Observed validation on macOS: Node 24.19.0 full storage suite passed 104 tests with four test processes. A subsequent focused store run passed 22 tests after adding manifest/containment checks. Changed-file ESLint passed. `verify:fast` passed before the last manifest/containment edits; it does not yet verify the final source identity.

A preceding full run passed 101/102 tests and timed out waiting for one contention worker readiness marker. Isolated contention passed. Worker readiness now reports early exits with diagnostics; the original timeout cause remains unresolved. Do not present this as a proven scheduling defect.

The original plan remains incomplete: no sole-writer cutover, full consumer migration, obsolete-code removal, release compatibility boundary, public maintenance suite, platform matrix, final reproducible performance comparison, or net LOC result exists yet. Goal and ForgeLoop task remain active; no PR has been opened.

## Increment 3 — shared operational persistence and a complete lifecycle fixture

The previous goal turn made concrete progress (snapshot export and parity evidence). This increment moves shared domain persistence beyond the diagnosis-only capability; it does not establish public cutover or full-plan completion.

- Added an internal project-bound operational preparation scope. Existing domain transaction callbacks gather validated proposed records outside a database transaction; a short synchronous `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` rechecks observed records and commits descriptors, conditional state updates, claims, structured artifacts and ledger events together. Callbacks and external operations are never replayed when claim candidates are refreshed.
- Canonical task creation writes descriptor, reservations and its commit witness directly to SQLite. Selected direct task lookup uses the task-key index rather than project-wide discovery. Domain claim classification, schema checks, preflight, receipt preparation, lifecycle transitions and completion validation remain shared with filesystem dispatch.
- A task created in SQLite now completes the actual discovery/contract/route/preflight/activation/plan/execution/verification/diagnosis/correction/review/completion sequence, including real failed/passing checker processes and shared schema tests. The fixture also abandons and resumes the task through canonical commands. Completion returns `VALID`; no legacy task or session directory is created. This is fixture completion only, not completion of this migration task.
- Session selection is a canonical database reference (schema 3, storage format 1). Snapshot export/import session coverage still needs implementation.
- Two independent prepared task creations reject overlapping ancestor claims and both accept non-overlapping claims. A changed catalogue triggers fresh shared claim validation before reservation, rather than re-executing the command.
- Action and approval domain services now use the record store. Idempotency lookup uses its unique index (confirmed with `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN`); immutable identity drift, already-resolved approval decisions and extracted-column tampering reject.
- Short domain mutations use no filesystem lock. External `run-check`/`run-action` work uses a durable operation reservation with shared lease identity/staleness rules. Intent/action transitions and outcomes can commit before/after launch without holding a writer during the external operation. Another runtime cannot mutate the reserved task, while unrelated task creation remains possible. Full action-launch crash/reconciliation tests remain required.
- Stale reservation removal uses compare-and-delete; malformed leases are not released by stale-only cleanup. Explicit force release remains available through the authorized command boundary.
- Claim release/reacquisition projections are updated in the same commit as canonical recovery/resume/completion evidence. Historical descriptor claims and ledger events remain retained.

Observed checks: the Node 24.19.0/macOS storage regression passed 114 tests before the final lease-release/recovery additions. The latest focused shared-operational suite passed 10 tests after the recovery and retained-handle additions. The default filesystem domain regression passed 138 tests. Fast verification passed after the optional lock-reference adjustment and before the final preparation-handle guard; that guard is covered by the focused suite. Changed-file ESLint has no errors; inherited complexity warnings remain.

The selected operational context is still internal for differential testing. Production still defaults to filesystem. All remaining public consumers, maintenance/cutover, attachment/export coverage, runtime/platform declarations, full regressions, old-client exclusion, obsolete-code deletion and original performance/LOC gates remain mandatory. No PR or release has been created.

Runtime observed for this increment: Node 24.19.0, bundled SQLite 3.53.3, macOS arm64. Cross-platform compatibility and the final minimum patch decision are still pending. Preparation observations use unambiguous composite identities, and a retained closed handle cannot write through a later transaction.

- Integration evaluation and semantic-decision resources enumerate canonical SQLite artifact identities instead of requiring legacy directories. Handoff and structural-quality evaluation readers use the same collection boundary and retain their existing schema and semantic validation.
- Nested collection enumeration returns direct members, includes proposed transaction artifacts, and observes the collection query so a concurrent insertion rejects the prepared mutation without publishing its proposed artifacts.
- Structural-quality evaluation immutability and attestation statement/history presence checks now read the selected operational store.

Tests prove absent legacy directories, resource payload parity, malformed canonical record rejection, nested collection containment and concurrent insertion conflict. Changed-file ESLint has zero errors and three existing domain complexity warnings.

This increment does not switch production dispatch, cover every integration consumer, or complete the original plan. Full evidence remains NOT_VERIFIED; migration task stays VERIFYING and no PR has been opened.

## Increment 12 — public project storage selection

- The shared executor registry and integration-resource boundary now select an existing canonical `.forgeloop/state.sqlite` without actor-controlled backend input or implicit conversion. Selection remains lazy; legacy projects without a database retain temporary development compatibility until controlled cutover.
- Mixed SQLite/legacy task-state, singleton state/ledger or transaction directories refuse with `E_STORAGE_MIGRATION_REQUIRED`. Path containment applies before opening. Resource reads and commands declared READ_ONLY open read-only connections; connections close after the call.
- Node 24.19.0/macOS combined operational/integration tests passed 28 checks before the final read-only selection refinement; the latest operational suite passed 16 checks afterward, including a real CLI child process reading a SQLite-only task and API/resource mixed-state refusal (`/tmp/sqlite-project-cli.log`). Fast verification passed before the final CLI assertion/read-only refinement. Changed-file lint previously had zero errors and one inherited resource complexity warning.

This is automatic selection for an existing store, not complete production cutover. Fresh-store policy, persistent-runtime connection reuse, every public consumer, maintenance/exclusion/restore, removal of filesystem writers and final original acceptance gates remain unfinished. Full migration remains NOT_VERIFIED and no PR exists.

## Increment 13 — read-only and SQLite sidecar containment

- Public project selection validates database WAL/shared-memory sidecar paths before SQLite can open them. Symlinked database or WAL paths refuse.

- Node 24.19.0/macOS operational tests passed 17 checks (`/tmp/sqlite-boundary-readonly.log`); changed-file lint passed. The first run had an incorrect expected error in the new test; the assertion was corrected to the existing explicit-upgrade error and rerun successfully.

## Increment 14 — native database backup and combined checks

- Added a database-only backup primitive using the native SQLite backup API, integrity checking before publication, file sync, exclusive hard-link publication and the existing portable directory-sync policy. It includes committed WAL data and refuses uncommitted snapshots and existing destinations. Attachment backup/restore is explicitly not included.
- Focused storage tests passed 27 checks before the final directory-sync addition (`/tmp/sqlite-native-backup.log`); changed-file lint passed.
- Fast verification passed with the public boundary. The concurrent full storage run had two failures: an obsolete assertion forbidding the new lazy boundary and a worker readiness timeout. The assertion now permits only the lazy boundary/context while still forbidding SQLite resolution on legacy dispatch. Worker readiness now reports early exit diagnostics. The focused evidence/contention rerun passed 14 tests (`/tmp/sqlite-public-recheck.log`); the original readiness timeout cause remains unproven.

Full-plan completion remains unverified. This primitive does not yet constitute the required public maintenance commands, backup/attachment restore drill, cutover procedure or sole-writer release.

## Increment 15 — database restore and startup contention diagnosis

- Added database-only restore into a fresh destination, preserving retained backups and refusing overwrite. Source metadata opens read-only, so unsupported backup formats are not silently upgraded. Attachment-aware restore and public maintenance commands remain unfinished.
- The full regression rerun exposed actual startup `E_STORAGE_BUSY` failures before worker readiness. Diagnostics showed migration initialization reserving the writer on every open, including current schemas. Current-schema opens now validate metadata without migration writer reservation; the busy timeout is established before WAL configuration.
- A deterministic regression opens a second current-schema connection with timeout zero while the first owns `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`. Node 24.19.0/macOS combined store/contention/correction tests passed 35 checks (`/tmp/sqlite-startup-contention.log`) after the fix, including restore round trip and retained destination refusal. Changed-file lint passed.

The earlier broad run failed and is not claimed green. Full original-plan completion remains NOT_VERIFIED; no PR exists.

## Increment 16 — ledger maintenance integrity

- Storage integrity streams ordered event rows, checking extracted fields, task identity, contiguous sequence, hash links, content hashes and known event details. Ledger findings are capped at 100; this check does not replace full domain chronology/authority validation.
- Database backup/restore consequently rejects tampered canonical event columns even when SQLite page/FK integrity passes. Import now verifies each canonical content hash as well as chain links.
- The prior lightweight interchange fixture used synthetic hashes; it now builds real canonical hashes. Node 24.19.0/macOS storage-store suite passed 29 checks (`/tmp/sqlite-backup-ledger.log`) after both fixes. Changed-file lint passed. Broad regression has not been rerun for this increment.

Full migration remains incomplete and active; no PR exists.

## Increment 19 — isolated bundle readback and versioned file bindings

- The completed SQLite fixture copies only its exported bundle into a separate directory and validates it without the database, task namespace or source inputs.
- New bundles use schema version 2, binding every included artifact to its size and SHA-256. File hashing streams bytes and rejects unsafe portable paths. Readback checks exact membership and file bytes before existing schema/protocol checks. Version 1 manifests remain readable.
- A changed exported ledger rejects with `E_BUNDLE_DIGEST_INVALID`. Existing tamper assertions were updated to accept earlier byte-binding rejection while retaining sensitive-value non-disclosure assertions.
- Node 24.19.0/macOS operational, bundle, structural-quality bundle and schema-golden checks passed (`/tmp/sqlite-bundle-v2.log`). Broad verification and generated-reference review remain pending for this increment.

Full migration remains unfinished and active; no PR exists. Bundle digests bind exported bytes but do not establish an independent external trust anchor.

## Increment 28 — retained source capture and maintenance exclusion

- Added internal capture and verification helpers for named operational roots, legacy singleton artifacts, sessions, transaction history, portable session bindings and in-layout raw attachments. Capture requires explicit operator quiescence, refuses retained locks/incomplete transactions, preserves source bytes and retained captures, streams copying/digests, synchronizes files/directories and checks membership/digests before marking `CAPTURED`. Unsupported source links and overlapping destinations are refused.
- Added owner-bound maintenance exclusion to public command/resource boundaries and filesystem task/claim acquisition. Nested work joins the same live owner; closed asynchronous contexts cannot reuse it. A real killed child leaves a retained exclusion, and later commands refuse automatic bypass. This proves fail-closed interruption, not implemented resume/publication.
- Node 24.19.0/macOS final capture/exclusion suite passed 7 tests (`/tmp/sqlite-maintenance-crash.log`). Earlier combined capture/operational regression passed 23 tests (`/tmp/sqlite-source-maintenance.log`); maintenance/task-lock/claim-lock/transaction regression passed 35 (`/tmp/sqlite-maintenance-locks.log`). Fast verification passed before the final source-root expansion and added crash test (`/tmp/sqlite-maintenance-fast.log`). Changed-file lint reported zero errors/warnings.

No project was converted. A marker cannot exclude older binaries: operator shutdown remains required. Supported maintenance status/resume, crash-resumable database publication, full reference/attachment inventories and all original sole-writer/platform/performance/LOC gates remain unfinished. Full plan remains NOT_VERIFIED; no PR exists.

## Increment 30 — owner-bound internal maintenance resume

- Extracted the bounded, UTF-8-preserving maintenance owner reader for shared status/recovery use. New owners bind to a hostname. Resume requires exact owner identity and explicit operator quiescence, refuses live or unbound/remote owners, serializes adoption, rechecks retained owner bytes and archives previous ownership exclusively before publishing a new owner. Existing matching archives are reusable checkpoints; conflicting archives remain untouched and abort adoption.
- Ordinary dispatch remains excluded through recovery. Closed context reuse stays forbidden. Callers can retain exclusion on failure; resume always retains it when resumed work fails. Interrupted handoff markers remain fail-closed and are visible through public migration status. This internal primitive does not implement complete public resume or every cutover restart stage.

Final local embedded MCP checks passed 9 tests (`/tmp/sqlite-resume-final-mcp.log`) using the previously documented borrowed dependencies/current-core loader.
- Earlier combined operational/lock/transaction/resume regression passed 56 tests (`/tmp/sqlite-resume-regression.log`) and fast verification passed (`/tmp/sqlite-resume-fast.log`) before final archive/handoff diagnostics. Final changed-file lint had zero errors/warnings. These earlier broad results are not mislabeled as tests of the final identity.

Public resume orchestration, complete handoff/publication crash resumption, canonical source/candidate parity, external attachment references, sole-writer removal and original runtime/platform/performance/LOC gates remain unfinished. ## Increment 39 — independent closed publication staging

## Increment 52 — recovery of recorded incomplete source capture

- Public recovery now validates CAPTURING/FAILED inventory against unchanged active sources before candidate recovery. Idle legacy locks/transactions and absence of candidate/staging/cutover state are required. Valid copies are reused, missing copies are exclusive and fsynced, and mismatched partial copies are retained under an exclusive `source-history/<uuid>/partial/...` path before copying originals. The prior manifest is retained. Final active and captured inventories must match before publishing CAPTURED.
- Retained-source inspection now enumerates the entire owned source tree and refuses unrecorded files outside known source roots; completed-capture verification uses the same check. ## Increment 71 — bounded catalog and backup-manifest reads
